Several Kurdish tutors were [forcibly disappered](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forcibly_disappeared) or subjected to prolonged imprisonments under charges of fomenting "separatism", treason, and undermining the stability of the Ba'athist state.[^cite_note-295]

Between 2011 and 2013, the Ba'athist state security apparatus tortured and killed over 10,000 [civil activists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_activist), [political dissidents](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_dissidents), [journalists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independent_journalists), [civil defense volunteers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_defense) and those accused of treason and terror charges, as part of a campaign of deadly crackdown ordered by Assad.[^cite_note-Entous,_Nissenbaum-296] In June 2023, [UN General Assembly](31957.md) voted in favour of establishing an independent body to investigate the whereabouts of hundreds of thousands of missing civilians who have been forcibly disappeared, killed or languishing in Assad regime's [dungeons](78548501.md#Detention_centers) and [torture chambers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Torture_chamber). The vote was condemned by Russia, North Korea and Iran.[^cite_note-297][^cite_note-298][^cite_note-299]

In 2023, [Canada](5042916.md) and Netherlands [filed a lawsuit against Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canada_and_the_Netherlands_v._Syrian_Arab_Republic) at the [International Court of Justice](14918.md) (ICJ), charging the latter with violating the [United Nations Convention Against Torture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Convention_Against_Torture). The joint petition accused the Syrian regime of organizing "unimaginable physical and mental pain and suffering" as a strategy to collectively punish the Syrian population.[^cite_note-300][^cite_note-301][^cite_note-302] Russia vetoed [UN Security Council](31956.md) efforts to prosecute Bashar al-Assad at the International Criminal Court.[^cite_note-303]

### Repression of Kurds

Further information: [Arab Belt Project](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_Belt), [Arabization in Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabization#Arabization_in_Syria), and [Qamishli massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qamishli_massacre)

Ba'athist Syria had long banned [Kurdish language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kurdish_language) in schools and public institutions; and discrimination against [Kurds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_Kurds) steadily increased during the rule of Bashar al-Assad. State policy officially suppressed Kurdish culture; with more than 300,000 Syrian Kurds being rendered stateless. Kurdish grievances against state persecution eventually culminated in the [2004 Qamishli Uprisings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2004_Qamishli_riots), which were crushed down violently after sending Syrian military forces. The ensuing crackdown resulted in the killings of more than 36 Kurds and injuring at least 160 demonstrators. More than 2000 civilians were arrested and tortured in government detention centres. Restrictions on Kurdish activities were further tightened following the Qamishli massacre, with the [Assad regime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assad_regime) virtually banning all Kurdish cultural gatherings and political activism under the charges of "inciting strife" or "weakening national sentiment". During 2005–2010, [Human Rights Watch](46530.md) verified security crackdowns on at least 14 [Kurdish](3979351.md) political and cultural gatherings.[^cite_note-hrw\.org-284][^cite_note-Hill-283] In March 2008, Syrian military opened fire at a Kurdish gathering in Qamishli that marked Nowruz, killing three and injuring five civilians.[^cite_note-304]

### Censorship

Main articles: [Censorship in Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Censorship_in_Syria) and [Internet censorship in Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internet_censorship_in_Syria)

On 22 September 2001, Assad decreed a Press Law that tightened government control over all literature printed or published in Syria; ranging from newspapers to books, pamphlets and periodicals. Publishers, writers, editors, distributors, journalists and other individuals accused of violating the Press Law are imprisoned or fined. Censorship has also been expanded into the [cyberspace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cyberspace), and various websites are banned. Numerous bloggers and content creators have been arrested under various "national security" charges.[^cite_note-hrw\.org-284]

A 2007 law requires [internet cafés](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internet_caf%C3%A9s) to record all the comments users post on chat forums.[^cite_note-305] Another decree in 2008 obligated internet cafes to keep records of their customers and report them routinely to the police.[^cite_note-10_Worst_Countries_to_be_a_Blogger-306] Websites such as [Arabic Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic_Wikipedia), [YouTube](3524766.md), and [Facebook](7529378.md) were [blocked intermittently between 2008 and February 2011](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internet_censorship_in_Syria).[^cite_note-307][^cite_note-308][^cite_note-309] [Committee to Protect Journalists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Committee_to_Protect_Journalists) (CPJ) ranked Syria as the third dangerous country to be an online blogger in 2009. Individuals are arrested based on a wide variety of accusations; ranging from undermining "national unity" to posting or sharing "false" content.[^cite_note-hrw\.org-284][^cite_note-10_Worst_Countries_to_be_a_Blogger-306]

Syria was ranked as the third most censored country in CPJ's 2012 report. Apart from restrictions for international journalists that prohibit their entry, domestic press is controlled by state agencies that promote Ba'athist ideology. From 2011, the Syrian government has issued a complete [media blackout](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media_blackout) and foreign correspondents were quickly detained, abducted or tortured. As a result, the outside world is able to know of situations happening inside Syria only through videos of independent civilian journalists. The Assad government has shut down internet coverage, [mobile networks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cellular_network) as well as telephone lines in areas under its control to prevent any news that has its attempts to monopolise information related to Syria.[^cite_note-310]

### Crackdowns, ethnic cleansing, and forced disappearances

Further information: [Casualties of the Syrian civil war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casualties_of_the_Syrian_civil_war) and [Syrian refugee crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_refugee_crisis)

The [crackdown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Syrian_revolution#Crackdown) ordered by Bashar al-Assad against Syrian protesters was the most ruthless of all military clampdowns in the entire [Arab Spring](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_Spring). As violence deteriorated and death toll mounted to the thousands; the European Union, Arab League and United States began imposing wide range of sanctions against Assad regime. By December 2011, [United Nations](31769.md) had declared the situation in Syria to be a "[civil war](7085.md)".[^cite_note-311] By this point, all the protestors and armed [resistance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Resistance_movement) groups had viewed the unconditional resignation of Bashar al-Assad as part of their core demands. In July 2012, [Arab League](52625.md) held an emergency session demanding the "swift resignation" of Assad and promised "safe exit" if he accepted the offer.[^cite_note-312][^cite_note-313] Assad rebuffed the offers, instead seeking foreign military support from Iran and Russia to defend his embattled regime through [scorched-earth tactics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scorched_earth), massacres, sieges, forced starvations, ethnic cleansing, etc.[^cite_note-314]

The crackdowns and extermination campaigns of Assad regime resulted in the [Syrian refugee crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_refugee_crisis); causing the [forced displacement](477535.md) of 14 million Syrians, with around 7.2 million refugees.[^cite_note-Syria_Refugee_Crisis_Explained-315] This has made the Syrian refugee crisis the largest [refugee crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Refugee_crisis) in the world; and [UNHCR](31964.md) High Commissioner [Filippo Grandi](24484119.md) has described it as "the biggest humanitarian and refugee crisis of our time and a continuing cause for suffering."[^cite_note-Syria_Refugee_Crisis_Explained-315][^cite_note-\:0-316]

### Ethnic cleansing



Wounded civilians getting rushed to a hospital in [Aleppo](159244.md)

Eva Koulouriotis has described Bashar al-Assad as the "master of [ethnic cleansing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnic_cleansing) in the 21st century".[^cite_note-J._Koulouriotis-317] During the course of the civil war, Assad ordered depopulation campaigns throughout the country to re-shape its demography in favor of his regime and the military tactics have been compared to the [persecutions of the Bosnian war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnic_cleansing_in_the_Bosnian_War). Between 2011 and 2015, [Ba'athist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ba'athism) militias are reported to have committed 49 ethno-sectarian [massacres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massacre) for the purpose of implementing its social engineering agenda in the country. [Alawite](436616.md) loyalist militias known as the *[Shabiha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shabiha)* have been launched into [Sunni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunni_Islam) villages and towns, perpetrating numerous [anti-Sunni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-Sunnism) massacres. These include the [Houla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houla_massacre), [Bayda and Baniyas massacres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bayda_and_Baniyas_massacres), [Al-Qubeir massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Qubeir_massacre), Al-Hasawiya massacre, and others which have resulted in hundreds of deaths with hundreds of thousands of residents fleeing under threats of regime persecution and [sexual violence](731637.md). Pogroms and deportations were pronounced in central Syrian regions and Alawite majority coastal areas where the Syrian military and Hezbollah prioritise the establishment of strategic control by expelling Sunni residents and bringing in Iran-backed Shia militants.[^cite_note-318][^cite_note-319][^cite_note-J._Koulouriotis-317][^cite_note-320] In 2016, UN officials criticised Bashar al-Assad for pursuing demographic engineering and ethnic cleansing in [Darayya district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Darayya_District) in Damascus under the guise of de-escalation deals.[^cite_note-321]

### War crimes

Further information: [War crimes in the Syrian civil war](36355222.md) and [Use of chemical weapons in the Syrian civil war](39668667.md)

> "The nature and extent of Assad's violence is strategic in design and effect. He is pursuing a [strategy of terror](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_terrorism), [siege](26905.md), and [depopulation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnic_cleansing) in key areas, calculating that winning back the loyalty of much of the [Sunni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunni) middle class and underclass is highly unlikely and certainly not worth the resources and political capital. Better to level half the country than to give it over to the [opposition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_opposition_to_Bashar_al-Assad)."

— Emile Hokayem, Senior Fellow at [International Institute for Strategic Studies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Institute_for_Strategic_Studies)

[^cite_note-322]

Syrian government forces have pursued mass-killings of civilian populations as part of its war strategy throughout the conflict and is responsible for inflicting more than 90% of the [total civilian deaths in the Syrian civil war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casualties_of_the_Syrian_civil_war).[^cite_note-auto1-323] The UN estimates a minimum of 306,000 civilian deaths occurred between 2011 and 2021.[^cite_note-Farge-127][^cite_note-ohchr.org-128] As of 2022, the total death toll has risen to approximately 580,000.[^cite_note-GCR2P2-324] An additional 154,000 civilians have been [forcibly disappeared](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forcibly_disappeared) or subject to [arbitrary detentions](13435705.md) across Syria between 2011 and 2023. As of 2023, more than 135,000 individuals are being [tortured](47702.md), incarcerated or dead in [Ba'athist prison networks](78548501.md#Detention_Centers), including thousands of [women](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Woman) and [children](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Child).[^cite_note-325]

In a December 2024 summary, the Syrian Network for Human Rights said that it had documented at least 202,000 civilians killed by Syrian government forces since March 2011, including more than 23,000 children. It also recorded 96,321 enforced disappearances and 15,102 deaths under torture during the same period.[^cite_note-326]

Since 2011, the Assad regime has arrested and detained children without trial until the age of 18, after which they are transferred to Syrian military field courts and killed. A 2024 investigative report by the Syrian Investigative Journalism Unit (SIRAJ) identified 24 Syrian children who were forcibly disappeared, had their assets confiscated, detained and later killed after they reached the age of 18. The report, based on inside sources within the Assad government, interviews with victims' families, and public sources, estimated that more than 6,000 detainees under the age of 18 were sentenced to death in the [Sednaya Prison](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sednaya_Prison) and an Assadist military field court in [Al-Dimas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Dimas) between 2014 and 2017, citing eyewitness accounts of an insider within the Ba'athist military police.[^cite_note-327][^cite_note-328]

Numerous politicians, dissidents, authors and journalists have nicknamed Assad as the "butcher" of Syria for his war-crimes, [anti-Sunni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-Sunnism) sectarian mass-killings, [chemical weapons attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chemical_warfare) and [ethnic cleansing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnic_cleansing) campaigns.[^cite_note-329][^cite_note-330][^cite_note-331][^cite_note-332] The [Federal Bureau of Investigation](11127.md) has stated that at least 10 European citizens were tortured by the Assad government while detained during the Syrian civil war, potentially leaving Assad open to prosecution by individual European countries for [war crimes](48626.md).[^cite_note-333][^cite_note-CNN_sarin_report-191] [UN High Commissioner for Human Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN_High_Commissioner_for_Human_Rights) Navi Pillay stated in December 2013 that UN investigations directly implicated Bashar al-Assad in perpetrating [crimes against humanity](61099.md) and pursuing an extermination strategy developed "at the highest level of government, including the [head of state](516106.md)."[^cite_note-334]

[Stephen Rapp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephen_Rapp), the [U.S. Ambassador-at-Large for War Crimes Issues](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Ambassador-at-Large_for_War_Crimes_Issues), stated in 2014 that the crimes committed by Assad are the worst seen since those of [Nazi Germany](21212.md).[^cite_note-335] In March 2015, Rapp further stated that the case against Assad is "much better" than those against [Slobodan Milošević](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slobodan_Milo%C5%A1evi%C4%87) of Serbia or [Charles Taylor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_Taylor_\(Liberian_politician\)) of Liberia, both of whom were indicted by international tribunals.[^cite_note-336] Charles Lister, Director of the Countering Terror and Extremism Program at [Middle East Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_East_Institute), describes Bashar al-Assad as the "21st century's biggest war criminal".[^cite_note-auto-202]



Bombing of [Darayya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Darayya) suburb of [Damascus](8914.md) by the [Syrian Arab Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_Air_Force), 17 June 2016

In a February 2015 interview with the [BBC](19344654.md), Assad dismissed accusations that the [Syrian Arab Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_Arab_Air_Force) used [barrel bombs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barrel_bomb) as "childish", claiming that his forces have never used these types of "barrel" bombs and responded with a joke about not using "cooking pots" either.[^cite_note-337] The *[BBC](19344654.md) Middle East* editor conducting the interview, [Jeremy Bowen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeremy_Bowen), later described Assad's statement regarding barrel bombs as "patently not true".[^cite_note-338][^cite_note-339] As soon as [demonstrations arose in 2011–2012](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_uprising_phase_of_the_Syrian_civil_war), Bashar al-Assad opted to implement the "[Samson option](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samson_Option)", the characteristic approach of the [Neo-ba'athist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neo-ba'athist) regime since the era of Hafez al-Assad; wherein protests were violently suppressed and demonstrators were shot and fired at directly by the armed forces. However, unlike Hafez; Bashar had even less loyalty and was politically fragile, exacerbated by alienation of the majority of the population. As a result, Bashar chose to crack down on dissent far more comprehensively and harshly than his father; and a mere allegation of collaboration was reason enough to get assassinated.[^cite_note-340]

[Nadim Shehadi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nadim_Shehadi), the director of [The Fares Center for Eastern Mediterranean Studies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Fares_Center_for_Eastern_Mediterranean_Studies), stated that "In the early 1990s, Saddam Hussein was massacring his people and we were worried about the weapons inspectors," and claimed that "Assad did that too. He kept us busy with chemical weapons when he massacred his people."[^cite_note-341][^cite_note-342] Contrasting the policies of Hafez al-Assad and that of his son Bashar, former [Syrian vice-president](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vice_President_of_Syria) and [Ba'athist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_Socialist_Ba'ath_Party_%E2%80%93_Syria_Region) dissident [Abdul Halim Khaddam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdul_Halim_Khaddam) states:&#x20;

> The Father had a mind and the Son has a loss of reason. How could the army use its force and the security apparatus with all its might to destroy Syria because of a protest against the mistakes of one of your security officials. The father would act differently. Father Hafez hit Hama after he encircled it, warned and then hit Hama after a long siege... But his son is different. On the subject of Daraa, Bashar gave instructions to open fire on the demonstrators.[^cite_note-343]

Human rights organisations and criminal investigators have documented Assad's war crimes and sent it to the [International Criminal Court](14880.md) for indictment.[^cite_note-344] Since Syria is not a party to the [Rome Statute](68762.md), [International Criminal Court](14880.md) requires authorisation from the [UN Security Council](31956.md) to send Bashar al-Assad to tribunal. As this gets consistently [vetoed](74661.md) by Assad's primary backer [Russia](25391.md), ICC prosecutions have not transpired. On the other hand, courts in various European countries have begun prosecuting and convicting senior Ba'ath party members, [Syrian military](27479.md) commanders and *[Mukhabarat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mukhabarat)* officials charged with war crimes.[^cite_note-345] In September 2015, France began an inquiry into Assad for [crimes against humanity](61099.md), with French Foreign Minister [Laurent Fabius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laurent_Fabius) stating "Faced with these crimes that offend the human conscience, this bureaucracy of horror, faced with this denial of the values of humanity, it is our responsibility to act against the impunity of the killers".[^cite_note-346]

In February 2016, head of the UN Commission of Inquiry on Syria, [Paulo Pinheiro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paulo_S%C3%A9rgio_Pinheiro), told reporters: "The mass scale of deaths of detainees suggests that the government of Syria is responsible for acts that amount to extermination as a crime against humanity." The UN Commission reported finding "unimaginable abuses", including women and children as young as seven perishing while being held by Syrian authorities. The report also stated: "There are reasonable grounds to believe that high-ranking officers – including the heads of branches and directorates – commanding these detention facilities, those in charge of the military police, as well as their civilian superiors, knew of the vast number of deaths occurring in detention facilities ... yet did not take action to prevent abuse, investigate allegations or prosecute those responsible".[^cite_note-347]

In March 2016, the [U.S. House Committee on Foreign Affairs](1267657.md) led by [New Jersey](21648.md) Rep. [Chris Smith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris_Smith_\(New_Jersey_politician\)) called on the Obama administration to create a war crimes [tribunal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tribunal) to investigate and prosecute violations "whether committed by the officials of the Government of Syria or other parties to the civil war".[^cite_note-348]

In June 2018, Germany's chief prosecutor issued an international arrest warrant for one of Assad's most senior military officials, [Jamil Hassan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jamil_Hassan).[^cite_note-349] Hassan is the head of Syria's powerful [Air Force Intelligence Directorate](19343088.md). Detention centers run by Air Force Intelligence are among the most notorious in Syria, and thousands are believed to have died because of [torture](47702.md) or neglect. Charges filed against Hassan claim he had command responsibility over the facilities and therefore knew of the abuse. The move against Hassan marked an important milestone of prosecutors trying to bring senior members of Assad's inner circle to trial for war crimes.

In an investigative report about the [Tadamon Massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tadamon_Massacre), Professors [Uğur Ümit Üngör](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U%C4%9Fur_%C3%9Cmit_%C3%9Cng%C3%B6r) and Annsar Shahhoud found witnesses who attested that Assad gave orders for the [Syrian Military Intelligence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_Intelligence_Directorate_\(Syria\)) to direct the [Shabiha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shabiha) to kill civilians.[^cite_note-350]

### 2023–2026 arrest warrant, legal proceedings and death sentence in absentia

See also: [Prosecution of Syrian civil war criminals](61708365.md)

On 15 November 2023, France issued an arrest warrant against Assad over the use of banned chemical weapons against civilians in Syria.[^cite_note-\:1-351] In May 2024, French anti-terrorism prosecutors requested the Paris appeals court to consider revoking Assad's arrest warrant, asserting his absolute immunity as a serving head of state.[^cite_note-\:3-352] On 26 June 2024, the Paris appeals court determined that the international arrest warrant issued by France against Assad for alleged complicity in war crimes during the Syrian civil war remains valid. This decision was confirmed by attorneys involved in the case, who said the ruling marked the first instance where a national court acknowledged that the personal immunity of a serving head of state is not absolute.[^cite_note-\:3-352] On 25 July 2025, the French [Court of Cassation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Court_of_Cassation_\(France\)) annulled Assad's arrest warrant, saying that he enjoyed [presidential immunity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidential_immunity) at the time of the attack, but allowed the investigations against him to continue and new warrants to be issued against him.[^cite_note-353]

On 20 January 2025, a French court issued an arrest warrant against Assad for the 2017 killing of 59-year old dual French-Syrian national Salah Abou Nabout in a bombing in [Deraa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deraa).[^cite_note-auto4-25] On 2 September 2025, a French court issued an arrest warrant against Assad for the 2012 killings of journalists [Marie Colvin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marie_Colvin) and [Remi Ochlik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Remi_Ochlik) in [Homs](77533.md).[^cite_note-auto3-27] On 27 September 2025, a Syrian court issued an arrest warrant for Assad over the [2011 Daraa incidents](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_Daraa), paving the way for its circulation through [Interpol](14338129.md) and international prosecution. The arrest warrant cited charges such as premeditated murder, torture leading to death, and deprivation of liberty, and was based on lawsuits submitted by the families of victims in [Daraa governorate](5317030.md) over events that occurred in November 2011.[^cite_note-354]

On 11 August 2026, the Fourth Criminal Court in Damascus sentenced Bashar al-Assad, his brother Maher al-Assad, former [defense minister](2774942.md) [Fahd Jassem al-Freij](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fahd_Jassem_al-Freij) and five other officials to [death in absentia](582826.md)—except for former head of the [Political Security Directorate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_Security_Directorate) in [Daraa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daraa) and his first cousin [Atef Najib](32651945.md), who was put in trial.[^cite_note-355][^cite_note-356][^cite_note-bbc-357][^cite_note-358] They were convicted of premeditated murder, torture and crimes against humanity committed during the Syrian Civil War.[^cite_note-359]

Following the ruling, a crowd gathered outside the court buildings, holding images of some of the people who had been killed under the Assad regime. There were spontaneous scenes of applause and chanting, while cars driving past, sounded their [vehicle horns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vehicle_horn) in support of the court's verdict.[^cite_note-bbc-357]

### Chemical attacks

Main article: [Use of chemical weapons in the Syrian civil war](39668667.md)

See also: [Syria and weapons of mass destruction](13895017.md)

The [Syrian military](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_Arab_Armed_Forces) has deployed chemical warfare as a systematic military strategy in the [Syrian civil war](30741795.md), and is estimated to have committed over 300 [chemical attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chemical_warfare), targeting civilian populations throughout the course of the conflict.[^cite_note-360][^cite_note-361] Investigation conducted by the [GPPi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Public_Policy_Institute_\(GPPi\)) research institute documented 336 confirmed attacks involving chemical weapons in Syria between 23 December 2012 and 18 January 2019. The study attributed 98% of the total verified chemical attacks to the Assad's regime. Almost 90% of the attacks had occurred after the [Ghouta chemical attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghouta_chemical_attack) in August 2013.[^cite_note-362][^cite_note-363]



Children killed by pro-[Assad military forces](27479.md) in the Ghouta chemical attack, the deadliest [chemical weapons attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chemical_warfare) in the 21st century

Syria joined the Chemical Weapons Convention and OPCW member state in October 2013, and there are currently three OPCW missions with UN mandates to investigate chemical weapons issues in Syria. These are the Declaration Assessment Team (DAT) to verify Syrian [declarations of CW Programme](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Destruction_of_Syria's_chemical_weapons); [OPCW Fact-Finding Mission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OPCW_Fact-Finding_Mission_in_Syria) (FFM) tasked to identify the chemical attacks and type of weapons used; and the Investigation and Identification Team (IIT) which investigates the perpetrators of the chemical attacks. The conclusions are submitted to the [United Nations bodies](31769.md).[^cite_note-364]

In April 2021, Syria was suspended from OPCW through the public vote of member states, for not co-operating with the body's Investigation Identification Team (IIT) and violating the [Chemical Weapons Convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chemical_Weapons_Convention).[^cite_note-365][^cite_note-366][^cite_note-367] Findings of another investigation report published the OPCW-IIT in July 2021 concluded that the Syrian regime had engaged in confirmed chemical attacks at least 17 times, out of the reported 77 chemical weapon attacks attributed to Assadist forces.[^cite_note-368][^cite_note-369] As of March 2023, independent United Nations inquiry commissions have confirmed at least nine chemical attacks committed by forces loyal to the [Assad government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assad_Government).[^cite_note-370][^cite_note-371]



Members of the Syrian community in [Hanover](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hanover) protest against Bashar al-Assad on the second anniversary of [Ghouta chemical attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghouta_chemical_attacks), 21 August 2015.

The deadliest chemical attack have been the Ghouta chemical attacks, when Assad government forces launched the [nerve agent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nerve_agent) [sarin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sarin) into civilian areas during its brutal [Siege of Eastern Ghouta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_Eastern_Ghouta) in early hours of 21 August 2013. Thousands of infected and dying victims flooded the nearby hospitals, showing symptoms such as foaming, body convulsions and other neurotoxic symptoms. An estimated 1,100–1,500 civilians; including women and children, are estimated to have been killed in the attacks.[^cite_note-372][^cite_note-373][^cite_note-374] The attack was internationally condemned and represented the deadliest use of chemical weapons since the [Iran-Iraq war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran%E2%80%93Iraq_War).[^cite_note-375][^cite_note-376] On 21 August 2022, United States government marked the ninth anniversary of Ghouta Chemical attacks stating: "[United States](3434750.md) remembers and honors the victims and survivors of the Ghouta attack and the many other chemical attacks we assess the Assad regime has launched. We condemn in the strongest possible terms any use of chemical weapons anywhere, by anyone, under any circumstances... The United States calls on the Assad regime to fully declare and destroy its [chemical weapons program](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syria_chemical_weapons_program)... and for the regime to allow the [Organisation for the Prohibition of Chemical Weapons](36832.md)' Declaration Assessment Team."[^cite_note-377]

In April 2017, a [sarin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sarin) [chemical attack on Khan Sheikhoun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khan_Shaykhun_chemical_attack) killed more than 80 people.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBronner200763-378][^cite_note-379][^cite_note-Entous,_Nissenbaum-296] In response, U.S. president [Donald Trump](4848272.md) ordered a [missile strike against the Syrian Shayrat base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Shayrat_missile_strike).[^cite_note-380][^cite_note-WhyWasSharyatAirbaseBombed-381] A joint report from the UN and international chemical weapons inspectors concluded that Assad regime perpetrated the sarin attack.[^cite_note-CNN_sarin_report-191][^cite_note-ReutersSyriaChemicalWeaponsKhanSheikhoun-382]

In April 2018, a [chemical attack occurred in Douma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Douma_chemical_attack), prompting the U.S. and its allies to accuse Assad of violating international law and initiated joint [missile strikes at chemical weapons facilities in Damascus and Homs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_bombing_of_Damascus_and_Homs). Both Syria and Russia denied involvement.[^cite_note-383][^cite_note-384] The third report published on 27 January 2023 by the OPCW-IIT concluded that the Assad regime was responsible for the [2018 Douma chemical attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Douma_chemical_attack) which killed at least 43 civilians.[^cite_note-385]

### Holocaust denial

See also: [Holocaust denial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holocaust_denial)

In a speech delivered at the [Ba'ath party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_Socialist_Ba'ath_Party_%E2%80%93_Syria_Region)'s central committee meeting in December 2023, Bashar al-Assad claimed that there was "no evidence" of the killings of six million Jews during [the Holocaust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Holocaust). Emphasising that Jews were not the sole [victims of Nazi extermination campaigns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holocaust_victims), Assad alleged that the Holocaust was "politicized" by [Allied powers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allied_Powers_\(World_War_II\)) to facilitate the mass-deportation of [European Jews](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Jews) to Palestine, and that it was used as an excuse to justify the creation of Israel. Assad also accused the U.S. government of financially and militarily sponsoring the [rise of Nazism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rise_of_Nazism) during the [inter-war period](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interwar_period).[^cite_note-386][^cite_note-387]

### Post-presidency revelations

In December 2025, leaked footage, originally filmed during a 2018 car tour in Eastern [Ghouta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghouta), obtained by [Al Arabiya](321645.md) showed Assad reportedly saying "to hell with Ghouta" while speaking with [Luna al-Shibl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luna_al-Shibl), a senior Syrian media adviser and former presidential spokesperson, which drew renewed outrage over the regime's record.[^cite_note-388]

According to a 2026 report in *[The Atlantic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Atlantic)*, former associates described him in later years as leading an increasingly insular life, spending time playing video games such as *[Candy Crush](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Candy_Crush_Saga)* and engaging in [extramarital relationships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Affair), including wives of former Syrian government officials. The article described him as stubborn and resistant to lifelines from allies, with his reliance on a small circle of confidants contributing to strategic misjudgments and a leadership disconnected from much of the Syrian population.[^cite_note-theatlantic-197]

## Public image



Uniformed Assad, reoccuring photo in propaganda images.

### Domestic opposition and support

Further information: [Syrian opposition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_opposition)



[Syrian opposition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_opposition_to_Bashar_al-Assad) in March 2013

The secular resistance to Assad rule is mainly represented by the [Syrian National Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_National_Council) and [National Coalition of Syrian Revolutionary and Opposition Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Coalition_of_Syrian_Revolutionary_and_Opposition_Forces), two political bodies that constitute a coalition of [centre-left](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centre-left_politics) and [right-wing](56522.md) [conservative](6675.md) factions of the [Syrian opposition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_opposition_to_Bashar_al-Assad). Military commanders and civilian leaders of [Free Syrian Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_Syrian_Army) militias are represented in these councils. The coalition represents the political wing of the [Syrian Interim Government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_Interim_Government) and seeks the [democratic transition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_transition) of Syria through grass-roots activism, protests and armed [resistance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Resistance_movement) to overthrow the Ba'athist dictatorship.[^cite_note-nahex-389][^cite_note-cnn-390][^cite_note-391] A less influential faction within the Syrian opposition is the [National Coordination Committee for Democratic Change](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Coordination_Committee_for_Democratic_Change) (NCC), a coalition of [left-wing](18499.md) [socialist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialism) parties that seek to end the rule of [Assad family](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Assad_family) but without [foreign involvement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_involvement_in_the_Syrian_civil_war). Established in June 2011, major parties in the NCC coalition are the [Democratic Arab Socialist Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Arab_Socialist_Union), [Syrian Democratic People's Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_Democratic_People's_Party) and the [Communist Labour Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communist_Labour_Party_\(Syria\)).[^cite_note-Guide_to_the_Syrian_opposition-392]

[National Democratic Rally](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Democratic_Rally_\(Syria\)) (NDR) was an older left-wing opposition coalition of [socialist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialism) parties formed in 1980, but banned by the Baathist government. NDR was active during the nationwide protests of the 1980s and the [Damascus Spring](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Damascus_Spring) of the 2000s.[^cite_note-393] During the early years of the civil war, the [Druze in Syria](47104446.md) primarily sought to remain neutral, "seeking to stay out of the conflict". [Druze-Israeli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Druze-Israeli) politician Majalli Wahabi claimed in 2016 that over half support the Assad government despite its relative weakness in Druze areas.[^cite_note-394] The "[Men of Dignity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Men_of_Dignity)" movement, which had sought to remain neutral and to defend Druze areas,[^cite_note-395] blamed the government after its leader [Sheikh Wahid al-Balous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sheikh_Wahid_al-Balous) was assassinated and organised large scale protests which left six government security personnel dead.[^cite_note-396] Druze community became fervently opposed to the Assad government over time and has been vocal about its opposition to increasing Iranian interference in Syria.[^cite_note-Abdulhamid-397] In August 2023, mass protests against Assad regime erupted in the Druze-majority city of [Suweida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/As-Suwayda),[^cite_note-398][^cite_note-Sinjab-399] which eventually spread to other [regions of Southern Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Syria_protests_\(2023_%E2%80%93_present\)).[^cite_note-Sinjab-399][^cite_note-400][^cite_note-401] Druze cleric Hikmat al-Hajiri, religious leader of Syrian Druze community, has declared war against "Iranian invasion of the country".[^cite_note-Abdulhamid-397] Syrian [Sufi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sufism) scholar [Muhammad al-Yaqoubi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhammad_al-Yaqoubi), a fervent opponent of both the Ba'athist regime and [Islamic State](9087364.md) group, has described Assad's rule as a "reign of terror" that wreaked havoc and enormous misery on the Syrian populace.[^cite_note-402] The regime's support base consisted of [Ba'athist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ba'athism) loyalists who dominate Syrian politics, [trade unions](17626.md), youth organisations, [students unions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Students'_union), [bureaucracy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bureaucracy) and [armed forces](27479.md).[^cite_note-403] [Ba'ath party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_Socialist_Ba'ath_Party_%E2%80%93_Syria_Region) institutions and its political activities form the "vital pillars of regime survival". Family networks of politicians in the Ba'ath party-led [National Progressive Front](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Progressive_Front_\(Syria\)) (NPF) and businessmen loyal to the Assad family form another pole of support. Electoral listing is supervised by Ba'ath party leadership which expels candidates not deemed "sufficiently loyal".[^cite_note-404][^cite_note-405][^cite_note-406] Although it has been reported at various stages of the Syrian civil war that [religious minorities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religion_in_Syria) such as the [Alawites](436616.md) and [Christians in Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christianity_in_Syria) favour the Assad government because of its secularism,[^cite_note-407][^cite_note-408] opposition exists among [Assyrian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assyrian_people) Christians who have claimed that the Assad government seeks to use them as "puppets" and deny their distinct ethnicity, which is non-Arab.[^cite_note-409] Although Syria's [Alawite](436616.md) community forms Bashar al-Assad's core support base and dominate the [military](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_Military) and [security apparatus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_Intelligence_Directorate_\(Syria\)),[^cite_note-410][^cite_note-411] in April 2016, [BBC News](1139893.md) reported that Alawite leaders released a document seeking to distance themselves from Assad.[^cite_note-412]



Military situation, November 2023 – November 2024

[Kurdish Supreme Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kurdish_Supreme_Committee) was a coalition of 13 Kurdish political parties opposed to Assad regime. Before its dissolution in 2015, the committee consisted of [KNC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kurdish_National_Council) and [PYD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Union_Party_\(Syria\)).[^cite_note-Guide_to_the_Syrian_opposition-392] [Circassians in Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Circassians_in_Syria) have also become strong opponents of the regime as Ba'athist crackdowns and massacres across Syria intensified viciously; and members of Circassian ethnic minority have attempted to escape Syria, fearing persecution.[^cite_note-413] In 2014, the Christian [Syriac Military Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syriac_Military_Council), the largest Christian organisation in Syria, allied with the Free Syrian Army opposed to Assad,[^cite_note-SyriacMilitaryCouncil-414] joining other Syrian Christian militias such as the [Sutoro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sutoro) who had joined the Syrian opposition against the Assad government.[^cite_note-415] [Ahmed al-Sharaa](44007699.md), also known by his *[nom de guerre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nom_de_guerre),* Abu Mohammad al-Julani, commander of the [Tahrir al-Sham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tahrir_al-Sham) rebel militia, condemned Assad regime for converting Syria "into an ongoing earthquake the past 12 years", in the context of the [2023 Turkey–Syria earthquakes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Turkey%E2%80%93Syria_earthquakes).[^cite_note-416]

In June 2014, Assad won a disputed [presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Syrian_presidential_election) held in government-controlled areas (and boycotted in opposition-held areas[^cite_note-417] and Kurdish areas governed by the [Democratic Union Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Union_Party_\(Syria\))[^cite_note-418]) with 88.7% of the vote. Turnout was estimated to be 73.42% of eligible voters, including those in rebel-controlled areas.[^cite_note-419] The regime's electoral commission also disqualified millions of Syrian citizens displaced outside the country from voting.[^cite_note-420] Independent observers and academic scholarship unanimously describe the event as a [sham election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sham_election) organised to legitimise Assad's rule.[^cite_note-421][^cite_note-422][^cite_note-423] In his inauguration ceremony, Bashar denounced the opposition as "terrorists" and "traitors"; while attacking the [West](21208200.md) for backing what he described as the "fake [Arab spring](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_Spring)".[^cite_note-424]

*[Times of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Times_of_Israel)* reported that although various individuals interviewed in a "Sunni-dominated, middle-class neighborhood of central Damascus" exhibited fealty for Assad; it was not possible to discern the actual support for the regime due to the ubiquitous influence of the [secret police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_intelligence) in the society.[^cite_note-425] Ba'athist dissident [Abdul Halim Khaddam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdul_Halim_Khaddam) who had served as [Syrian Vice President](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vice_President_of_Syria) during the tenures of both Hafez and Bashar, disparaged Bashar al-Assad as a pawn in Iran's [imperial scheme](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shia_crescent). Contrasting the power dynamics that existed under both the autocrats, Khaddam stated:&#x20;

> \[Bashar] is not like his father.. \[Hafez] never allowed the Iranians to intervene in Syrian affairs.. During Hafez Assad's time, an Iranian delegation arrived in Syria and attempted to convert some of the Muslim Alawite Syrians to Shia Islam... Assad ordered his minister of foreign Affairs to summon the Iranian ambassador to deliver an ultimatum: The delegation has 24 hours to exit Syria.... They had no power \[during Hafez's rule], unlike Bashar who gave them \[Iranians] power and control.[^cite_note-426][^cite_note-427]

### International opposition



Anti-Assad demonstrations held in [Paris](22989.md), 14 December 2016

Foreign journalists and political observers who travelled to Syria have described it as the most "ruthless [police state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Police_state)" in the [Arab World](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_world). Assad's violent repression of [Damascus Spring](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Damascus_Spring) of the early 2000s and the publication of a UN report that implicated him in the [assassination](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assassination_of_Rafic_Hariri) of [Lebanese Prime Minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanese_Prime_Minister) [Rafic Hariri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafic_Hariri), exacerbated Syria's [post-Cold War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Post%E2%80%93Cold_War_era) isolation.[^cite_note-428][^cite_note-Fisher-429] Following global outrage against Assad regime's deadly crackdown on the [Arab Spring protestors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_Spring) which led to the Syrian civil war, [scorched-earth policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scorched_earth) against the civilian populations resulting in more than half a million deaths, [mass murders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mass_murder) and systematic deployment of [chemical warfare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chemical_warfare) throughout the conflict; Bashar al-Assad became an international [pariah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pariah_state) and numerous world leaders have urged him to resign.[^cite_note-430][^cite_note-Fisher-429][^cite_note-431][^cite_note-432]

Since 2011, Bashar al-Assad has lost recognition from several international organisations such as the [Arab League](52625.md) (in 2011),[^cite_note-NYT_Arab_League-433] [Union for the Mediterranean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_for_the_Mediterranean) (in 2011)[^cite_note-434] and [Organisation of Islamic Co-operation](47725.md) (in 2012).[^cite_note-CNN_OIC-435][^cite_note-436] [United States](3434750.md), [European Union](9317.md), [Turkey](11125639.md), [Arab League](52625.md) and various countries began enforcing broad sets of sanctions against Syrian regime from 2011, with the objective of forcing Assad to resign and assist in a political solution to the crisis.[^cite_note-437] International bodies have criticised one-sided elections organised by Assad government during the conflict. In the 2014 London conference of countries of the [Friends of Syria group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Friends_of_Syria_Group), [British Foreign Secretary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_Secretary_\(United_Kingdom\)) [William Hague](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Hague) characterised Syrian elections as a "parody of democracy" and denounced the regime's "utter disregard for human life" for perpetrating [war-crimes](48626.md) and [state-terror](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_terrorism) on the [Syrian population](8862873.md).[^cite_note-438] Assad's policy of holding elections under the circumstances of an ongoing civil war were also rebuked by the [UN Secretary-General](162415.md) [Ban Ki-moon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ban_Ki-moon).[^cite_note-UN-439]



Assad meets with U.S. Senator [Ted Kaufman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ted_Kaufman) in 2009

[Georgia](48768.md) suspended all relations with Syria following Bashar al-Assad's [recognition of Abkhazia and South Ossetia](19042397.md), condemning his government as a "Russian-manipulated regime" that supported [Russian occupation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian-occupied_territories_in_Georgia) and "[ethnic cleansing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnic_cleansing)".[^cite_note-443] Following Assad's strong backing of [Russian invasion of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine) and recognition of the breakaway separatist republics, [Ukraine](31750.md) cut off all diplomatic relations with Syria in June 2022. Describing Assad's policies as "worthless", [Ukrainian President](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainian_President) [Volodymyr Zelenskyy](59210523.md) pledged to expand further [sanctions against Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanctions_against_Syria).[^cite_note-444][^cite_note-445] In March 2023, [National Security and Defense Council of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Security_and_Defense_Council_of_Ukraine) put into effect a range of sanctions targeting 141 firms and 300 individuals linked to Assad regime, Russian weapons manufacturers and Iranian dronemakers. This was days after Assad's visit to [Moscow](19004.md), wherein he justified [Russian invasion of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine) as a fight against "old and new Nazis". Bashar al-Assad, Prime Minister [Hussein Arnous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hussein_Arnous) and Foreign Minister [Faisal Mikdad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Faisal_Mekdad) were amongst the individuals who were sanctioned.[^cite_note-451] Sanctions also involved freezing of all Syrian state properties in Ukraine, curtailment of monetary transactions, termination of economic commitments and recision of all official Ukrainian awards.[^cite_note-The_New_Arab-449] Syria formally broke its diplomatic ties to Ukraine on 20 July, citing the [principle of reciprocity](1046231.md).[^cite_note-452]



Anti-Assad demonstrations in [Berlin](3354.md), 18 March 2023

In April 2023, a French court declared three high-ranking Ba'athist security officials guilty of [crimes against humanity](61099.md), [torture](47702.md), and various war-crimes against French-Syrian citizens. These included [Ali Mamlouk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ali_Mamlouk), director of [National Security Bureau of Syrian Ba'ath party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Security_Bureau_of_the_Arab_Socialist_Ba'ath_Party_%E2%80%93_Syria_Region) and [Jamil Hassan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jamil_Hassan), former head of the [Syrian Air Force Intelligence Directorate](19343088.md).[^cite_note-453][^cite_note-454] France had issued [international arrest warrants](691894.md) against the three officers over the case in 2018.[^cite_note-455] In May 2023, [French Foreign Minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Foreign_Minister) Catherine Colonna publicly demanded the prosecution of Bashar al-Assad for engaging in [chemical warfare](39668667.md) and killing [hundreds of thousands of people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casualties_of_the_Syrian_civil_war); branding him as "the enemy of his own people".[^cite_note-456][^cite_note-457] On 15 November 2023, France issued an arrest warrant against Assad for use of chemical weapons against civilians in Syria.[^cite_note-\:1-351]

#### Left-wing



Pro-[Putin](32817.md), Pro-[soviet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_Union) and Pro-Assad protests in Russia, amidst the [Russian intervention in the Syrian Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_intervention_in_the_Syrian_civil_war).&#x20;

Bashar al-Assad is widely criticised by left-wing activists and intellectuals world-wide for appropriating leftist ideologies and its [socialist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist), [progressive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Progressivism) slogans as a cover for his own family rule and to empower a loyalist clique of elites at the expense of ordinary Syrians. His close alliance with clergy-ruled [Khomeinist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khomeinism) Iran and its sectarian militant networks while simultaneously pursuing a policy of locking up left-wing critics of [Assad family](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Assad_family) has been subject to heavy criticism.[^cite_note-458]

The [Egyptian branch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_Socialist_Ba'ath_Party_%E2%80%93_Egypt_Region) of the [Iraqi Ba'ath movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iraqi_Ba'ath_movement) has declared its strong support to the [Syrian revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Syrian_Revolution); denouncing Ba'athist Syria as a repressive dictatorship controlled by the "Assad gang". It has attacked Assad family's Ba'athist credentials, accusing the [Syrian Ba'ath party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_Socialist_Ba'ath_Party_%E2%80%93_Syria_Region) of acting as the borderguards of [Israel](9282173.md) ever since its overthrowal of the [Ba'athist National Command](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Command_of_the_Ba'ath_Party) during the [1966 coup d'état](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1966_Syrian_coup_d'%C3%A9tat). Describing Bashar al-Assad as a disgraceful person for inviting hostile powers like [Iran](14653.md) to Syria, Egyptian Ba'athists have urged the [Syrian revolutionaries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_Syrian_Army) to unite in their efforts to overthrow the Assad regime and resist foreign imperialism.[^cite_note-459]

&#x20;Describing Assad's regime as a [mafia state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mafia_state) that thrives on corruption and sectarianism, [Lebanese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanese_people) socialist academic [Gilbert Achcar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gilbert_Achcar) stated:&#x20;

> Bashar Assad's cousin became the richest man in the country, controlling – it is widely believed – over half of the economy. And that's only one member of the ruling clan... The clan functions as a real mafia, and has been ruling the country for several decades. This constitutes the deep root of the explosion, in combination with the fact that the Syrian regime is one of the most despotic in the region. Compared to Assad's Syria, [Mubarak's Egypt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Egypt_under_Hosni_Mubarak) was a beacon of democracy and political freedom!... What is specific to this regime is that Assad's father has reshaped and reconstructed the state apparatus, especially its hard nucleus – the armed forces – in order to create a Pretorian guard for itself. The army, especially its elite forces, is tied to the regime itself in various ways, most prominently through the use of sectarianism. Even people who had never heard of Syria before know now that the regime is based on one minority in the country – about 10% of the population; the [Alawites](436616.md).[^cite_note-460]

The [Progressive Socialist Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Progressive_Socialist_Party) (PSP) in Lebanon has taken an anti-Assad stance and organised mass-protests in support of the Syrian revolution. In August 2012, PSP publicly denounced the Assad government as a "killing machine" engaged in slaughtering Syrian people. PSP leader Ayman Kamaleddine demanded the expulsion of the Syrian ambassador from Lebanon, describing him as "the representative of the murderer regime in Lebanon".

### International support

In 2017, then-[Democratic](5043544.md) [congresswoman](19468510.md) [Tulsi Gabbard](26328774.md) met with then-president Bashar al-Assad on an unannounced visit to Syria.[^cite_note-\:4-461] The visit was the first by a U.S. lawmaker since 2011,[^cite_note-462] and made under a travel warning issued by the [United States Department of State](31975.md), which continues to warn U.S. citizens against all travel to the country.[^cite_note-\:5-463] Gabbard did not consult with the [House speaker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Speaker_of_the_United_States_House_of_Representatives) or State Department before meeting with Assad.[^cite_note-\:5-463] The meeting came after Gabbard introduced legislation that would, in her words, "end our country's illegal war to overthrow the Syrian government."[^cite_note-\:5-463] She said that Assad is "a brutal [dictator](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dictator). Just like [Saddam Hussein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saddam_Hussein). Just like [Gadhafi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gadhafi) in Libya. The reason that I'm so outspoken on this issue of ending these wasteful [regime-change](37258993.md) wars is because I have seen firsthand this high human cost of war and the impact that it has on my fellow brothers and sisters in uniform."[^cite_note-464] Following her visit to Syria, Gabbard expressed doubts that the Assad regime was behind chemical weapons attacks in Syria.[^cite_note-\:4-461][^cite_note-465] Gabbard has continued to defend her comments, and in 2019 stated that Assad was "not the enemy of the United States because Syria does not pose a direct threat to the United States".[^cite_note-\:4-461]

#### Left-wing

Left-wing support for Assad had been split since the start of the Syrian civil war;[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHashemiPostel201311–13-466] the Assad government was accused of cynically manipulating [sectarian identity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sectarianism_and_minorities_in_the_Syrian_Civil_War) and [anti-imperialism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-imperialism) to continue its worst activities.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHashemiPostel2013231-467]

Some heads of state or governments declared their support for Assad, including North Korean leader [Kim Jong Un](5962511.md).[^cite_note-468] After declaring victory in the 2014 elections, Assad received congratulations from [President of Venezuela](3329692.md) [Nicolás Maduro](7161476.md),[^cite_note-469] [President of Algeria](380664.md) [Abdelaziz Bouteflika](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdelaziz_Bouteflika),[^cite_note-470] [President of Guyana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_Guyana) [Donald Ramotar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Ramotar),[^cite_note-471] [President of South Africa](321700.md) [Jacob Zuma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jacob_Zuma),[^cite_note-472] President of [Nicaragua](21362.md) [Daniel Ortega](8778.md),[^cite_note-473] and [Mahmoud Abbas](232595.md), the leader of [Fatah](11104.md) and [President of the State of Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_the_State_of_Palestine).[^cite_note-474][^cite_note-475][^cite_note-476] Palestinian [Marxist–Leninist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marxism%E2%80%93Leninism) militant group [Popular Front for the Liberation of Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Popular_Front_for_the_Liberation_of_Palestine) (PFLP) supported Assad during the Syrian civil war. As a result of this stance, the Iranian government increased its military and financial funding to the PFLP.[^cite_note-477][^cite_note-478]

#### Right-wing

Bashar al-Assad's regime has received support from prominent [white nationalist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_nationalist), [neo-Nazi](54361.md) and [far-right](221220.md) figures in Europe, who were attracted by his "[war on terror](13425800.md)" discourse against [Islamists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamists) during the period of [European refugee crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_refugee_crisis). Assad's bombings of Syrian cities are admired in the [Islamophobic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamophobic) discourse of far-right circles, which considers Muslims as a civilisational enemy. American [white supremacists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White-supremacists) often praise Assad as an authoritarian bulwark against what they view as the forces of "Islamic extremism" and [globalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Globalist_\(epithet\)); and several pro-Assad slogans were chanted in the neo-Nazi [Unite the Right rally](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unite_the_Right_rally) held in [Charlottesville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charlottesville,_Virginia) in 2017.[^cite_note-479][^cite_note-\:8-480]

[Nick Griffin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nick_Griffin), the former leader of the [British National Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_National_Party) (BNP), was formerly an official ambassador and guest of the Syrian government;[^cite_note-481] due to public controversy, the Assad government publicly disassociated itself from him after his trip to Syria in 2014.[^cite_note-\:8-480]

### International public relations



Assad with Iranian President [Mahmoud Ahmadinejad](7907317.md) and Ayatollah Ali Khamenei in Tehran, Iran, 2010

In order to promote their image and media-portrayal overseas, Bashar al-Assad and his wife [Asma al-Assad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asma_al-Assad) hired U.S. and UK based [PR firms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_relations) and consultants.[^cite_note-turnedtowest-482] In particular, these secured photoshoots for Asma al-Assad with fashion and celebrity magazines, including *[Vogue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vogue_\(magazine\))*'s March 2011 "A Rose in the Desert".[^cite_note-483][^cite_note-484] These firms included [Bell Pottinger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bell_Pottinger) and [Brown Lloyd James](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brown_Lloyd_James), with the latter being paid \$5,000 a month for their services.[^cite_note-turnedtowest-482][^cite_note-PRcomeback-485]

At the outset of the Syrian civil war, Syrian government networks were hacked by the group [Anonymous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anonymous_\(group\)), revealing that an ex-[Al Jazeera](30204052.md) journalist had been hired to advise Assad on how to manipulate the public opinion of the U.S. Among the advice was the suggestion to compare the popular uprising against the regime to the [Occupy Wall Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Occupy_Wall_Street) protests.[^cite_note-486] In a separate email leak several months later by the [Supreme Council of the Syrian Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Council_of_the_Syrian_Revolution), which were published by *[The Guardian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Guardian)*, it was revealed that Assad's consultants had coordinated with an Iranian government media advisor.[^cite_note-487] In March 2015, an expanded version of the aforementioned leaks was handed to the Lebanese *[NOW News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NOW_News)* website and published the following month.[^cite_note-488]

After the Syrian civil war began, the Assads started a [social media](5897742.md) campaign which included building a presence on Facebook, YouTube, and most notably [Instagram](31591547.md).[^cite_note-PRcomeback-485] A Twitter account for Assad was reportedly activated; however, it remained unverified.[^cite_note-489] This resulted in much criticism, and was described by *[The Atlantic Wire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Atlantic_Wire)* as "a propaganda campaign that ultimately has made the \[Assad] family look worse".[^cite_note-490] The Assad government has also allegedly arrested activists for creating Facebook groups that the government disapproved of,[^cite_note-Dissent2014-155] and has appealed directly to Twitter to remove accounts it disliked.[^cite_note-491] The social media campaign, as well as the previously leaked emails, led to comparisons with [Hannah Arendt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hannah_Arendt)'s *[A Report on the Banality of Evil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eichmann_in_Jerusalem)* by *The Guardian*, *The New York Times* and the *Financial Times*.[^cite_note-492][^cite_note-493][^cite_note-494]

In October 2014, 27,000 photographs depicting torture committed by the Assad government were put on display at the [U.S. Holocaust Memorial Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Holocaust_Memorial_Museum).[^cite_note-495][^cite_note-496] Lawyers were hired to write a report on the images by the British law firm [Carter-Ruck](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carter-Ruck), which in turn was funded by the [Government of Qatar](25190.md).[^cite_note-497] In November 2014, the [Quilliam Foundation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quilliam_\(think_tank\)) reported that a propaganda campaign, which they claimed had the "full backing of Assad", spread false reports about the deaths of Western-born jihadists in order to deflect attention from the government's alleged war crimes. Using a picture of a Chechen fighter from the [Second Chechen War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Chechen_War), pro-Assad media reports disseminated to Western media outlets, leading them to publish a false story regarding the death of a non-existent British jihadist.[^cite_note-Simpson_2014-498]

In 2015, [Russia intervened in the Syrian civil war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_intervention_in_the_Syrian_civil_war) in support of Assad, and on 21 October 2015, Assad flew to Moscow and met with Russian president Vladimir Putin, who said regarding the civil war: "this decision can be made only by the Syrian people. Syria is a friendly country. And we are ready to support it not only militarily but politically as well."[^cite_note-499]

## Personal life



Bashar al-Assad and his wife [Asma al-Assad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asma_al-Assad)

Assad is an [Alawite](436616.md) Muslim.[^cite_note-500] He has performed the *[hajj](23487999.md)* pilgrimage twice, in 1999 and in 2000.[^cite_note-501] He speaks fluent English and conversational French, having studied at the Franco-Arab al-Hurriyah school in Damascus.[^cite_note-502]

Assad's sister, [Bushra al-Assad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bushra_al-Assad), and mother, [Anisa Makhlouf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anisa_Makhlouf), left Syria in 2012 and 2013, respectively, to live in the [United Arab Emirates](69328.md).[^cite_note-New_Republic_2013-42] Makhlouf died in Damascus in 2016.[^cite_note-aje-503]

In December 2000, Assad married [Asma Akhras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asma_al-Assad), a British citizen of Syrian origin from [Acton, London](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acton,_London).[^cite_note-504][^cite_note-505] In 2001, Asma gave birth to their first child, a son named [Hafez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hafez_Bashar_al-Assad). Hafez graduated from [Moscow State University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moscow_State_University) in the November 2024 with a [doctorate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doctorate) in [number theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Number_theory).[^cite_note-506] Their daughter Zein was born in 2003, followed by their son Karim in 2004.[^cite_note-New_Republic_2013-42] On 23 December 2024, it was reported that Asma al-Assad had filed for divorce; the Russian government denied this.[^cite_note-507][^cite_note-508]

Assad is under 24-hour protection from the [FSB](385990.md). He reportedly lived in apartments under the [Four Seasons Hotel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Four_Seasons_Hotel_Moscow), and in the [Federation Tower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federation_Tower), before being moved to Rublyovka.[^cite_note-Nyald-509]

In the nearly 50-minute speech, President al-Assad gave his interpretation to the protests in Syria and other Arab countries, almost mocking the Arab Spring, invoking the outside regional and international conspirators and their inside 'agents' as the evil 'them'.. Resorting to conspiratorial attribution to explain the 'real' reasons behind the protests in Syria, al-Assad bestowed a pathological inevitability on the actions of political opposition, where the oppressive political system he heads turns into a noble victim, recipient of foreign illnesses. \[Assad speech] "Conspiracies are like germs: They procreate at every moment everywhere; they cannot be annihilated, but the immunity of the body can be strengthened against them." … dissent loses its political meaning, or moral justification, acquiring 'othering' essence when the president places it in the dismissive context of the 'fitna'.
